Appendix I
Computable bound for
\mathcal{W}_{p}(W_{k},\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}\frac{k(n-k)}{n}))

In this subsection we present a computable upper bound for
\mathcal{W}_{p}(W_{k},\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}\frac{k(n-k)}{n}))
.

I.1
Notations

We first present some constants that appear in
lemma

I.1
.

\displaystyle D_{n,p}:=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{n}}\min\begin{cases}\sqrt{p-1}\max\Big((\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1)^{1-1/p},\Big[\frac{(\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1)^{p}+\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1}{\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}}\Big]^{1/p}\Big)\\
\max\Big((\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1)^{1-1/p},\Big[\frac{(\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1)^{p}\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1}{\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}}\Big]^{1/p}\Big)A^{*}_{n,p}+\sqrt{\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1}A_{p}\\
\sqrt{2}\Big(\frac{\Gamma(\frac{p+1}{2})}{\sqrt{\pi}}\Big)^{1/p}\tilde{R}_{s}^{2(1-2/p)}(\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1)^{1/p}\\
\sqrt{2}2^{-1/p}\Big(\frac{\Gamma(\frac{p+1}{2})}{\sqrt{\pi}}\Big)^{1/p}\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\|\textnormal{Binomial}(n,\frac{2(\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1)}{\tilde{R}_{s}^{4}})\|_{p/2}}\quad\ \text{if}\ p\geq 4.\end{cases}

Moreover we write

\displaystyle C_{n,p}:=\min\begin{cases}\tilde{A}_{n,p}\tilde{R}2^{1/p}+\sqrt{2}A_{n,p}\\
{\sqrt{2}}\Big(\frac{\Gamma(\frac{p+1}{2})}{\sqrt{\pi}}\Big)^{1/p}\begin{cases}2^{1/p}\tilde{R}^{1-2/p}\text{\quad if }p<4\\
2^{1/p}\tilde{R}^{1-2/p}\wedge\frac{\tilde{R}}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\|\textnormal{Binomial}(n,\frac{2}{\tilde{R}^{2}})\|_{p/2}}\quad\text{otherwise};\end{cases}\end{cases}

and

B_{p,n}:=\min\Big(\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n}\|\textnormal{Binomial}(n,\frac{2}{\tilde{R}^{2}})\|_{p},1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{U}_{n,p}\tilde{R}\Big).

I.2
Computable bound

Lemma I.1
.

Let
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
be an independent standard random variable.Let
p\geq 2
be a constant.
Then for all
k\leq n
we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{p}(W_{k},\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{n,k}^{2}))

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{S}_{p}^{\sigma,R}(n,k):=\sqrt{p-1}\sqrt{n}(R+\sigma)\wedge\begin{cases}\max\big(\tilde{\omega}_{p}^{R}(\sigma,k),\tilde{\omega}_{p}^{R}(\sigma,n-k))\penalty\ \rm{if}\penalty\ k\neq n/2\\
\frac{1}{2}\{\tilde{\omega}_{p}^{R}(\sigma,n)\}\penalty\ \textrm{otherwise}.\end{cases}

where

\displaystyle\sigma^{-1}\tilde{\omega}_{p}^{R}(\sigma,n)

\displaystyle:=\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\kappa\leq\tilde{R}^{2}/n\\
K_{p}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{\sqrt{n}}{M_{n,\kappa}\mathbb{I}(p\neq 2)+\mathbb{I}(p=2)}\Big\{\|Z\|_{p}\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}-\sin^{-1}(\sqrt{1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa}})\Big)+\|Z\|_{p}D_{n,p}M_{n,\kappa}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{B_{p,n}}{2}\Big\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K_{p}>j\geq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{R^{2k}}{\sqrt{n}}\Big(\frac{\|H_{2j+1}\|_{p}}{(2j+2)!}-\frac{2^{-j}e^{19/300}\pi^{1/4}K_{p}^{1/4}\sqrt{p-1}^{2k+1}}{2(K_{p}+1)\sqrt{2K_{p}+1}j!}\Big)\int_{{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{\kappa}}}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{y}}\Big(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n}\Big)^{j}dy

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{4}e^{19/300}\pi^{1/4}\frac{K_{p}^{1/4}\sqrt{p-1}}{(K_{p}+1)\sqrt{2K_{p}+1}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{\kappa}}}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{y}}\big(e^{\frac{1}{2}(p-1)\tilde{R}^{2}(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n})}-1\big)dy\Big\}

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{n,p}}{2}\Big\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq K_{p}-1\end{subarray}}\frac{\tilde{R}^{2j}}{n}\Big(\frac{\|H_{2j}\|_{p}}{(2j+1)!}-\frac{K_{p}^{1/4}2^{-j}(p-1)^{j}e^{19/300}\pi^{1/4}}{(2K_{p}+1)j!}\Big)\int_{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{\kappa}}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{y}}\big(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n}\big)^{j-\frac{1}{2}}dy

\displaystyle\quad+e^{19/300}\pi^{1/4}\frac{K_{p}^{1/4}}{(2K_{p}+1)n}\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{\frac{\kappa}{\tilde{R}^{2}}}\frac{1}{y^{3/2}\sqrt{y-\frac{1}{n}}}\big[e^{\frac{1}{2}(p-1)\tilde{R}^{2}\big(y-\frac{1}{n}\big)}-1\big]dy\Big\}\Big\},

where
M_{n,\kappa}:={\sqrt{1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa}}}
and
\sigma^{2}_{n,k}:=\frac{\sigma k(n-k)}{n}.

Proof.

If
k\neq n/2
then we first remark that

\displaystyle W_{k}=\Big(1-\frac{k}{n}\Big)S_{k}-\frac{k}{n}(S_{n}-S_{k}).

We note that
S_{k}
and
S_{n}-S_{k}
are two independent sums of i.i.d random variables. Hence according to the definition of the Wasserstein-p distance we know that for all
\epsilon>0
there exists
G_{1}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,k\sigma^{2})
and
G_{2}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,(n-k)\sigma^{2})
such that

\|S_{k}-G_{1}\|_{p}\leq\mathcal{W}_{p}(S_{k},\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}k))+\epsilon,\penalty\ \|S_{n}-S_{k}-G_{2}\|_{p}\leq\mathcal{W}_{p}(S_{n}-S_{k},\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}(n-k)))+\epsilon.

Moreover we remark that

\Big(1-\frac{k}{n}\Big)G_{1}-\frac{k}{n}G_{2}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{k(n-k)\sigma^{2}}{n}).

Hence we obtain that

\mathcal{W}_{p}(W_{k},\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}_{n,k}))\leq(1-\frac{k}{n})\mathcal{W}_{p}(S_{k},\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}k))+\frac{k}{n}\mathcal{W}_{p}(S_{n}-S_{k},\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}(n-k)))+2\epsilon.

As
\epsilon>0
is arbitrary we directly obtain that

\mathcal{W}_{p}(W_{k},\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}_{n,k}))\leq\Big(1-\frac{k}{n}\Big)\mathcal{W}_{p}(S_{k},\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}k))+\frac{k}{n}\mathcal{W}_{p}(S_{n}-S_{k},\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}(n-k))).

According to Lemma 8
[
2
]
we obtain that

\mathcal{W}_{p}(W_{k},\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}_{n,k}))\leq\max\Big(\tilde{\omega}_{p}^{R}(\sigma,k),\tilde{\omega}_{p}^{R}(\sigma,n-k)\Big).

Moreover using
lemma

K.3
we note that the following trivial bound also holds:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{p}(W_{k},\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{n,k}^{2}))

\displaystyle\leq\|W_{k}\|_{p}+\sigma_{n,k}\|Z\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{p-1}\Big((1-\frac{k}{n})R\sqrt{k}+\frac{k\sqrt{n-k}R}{n}+\sigma_{n,k}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{p-1}\frac{\sqrt{n}}{2}\Big(R\sqrt{2}+\sigma\Big).

We finally prove a tighter bound if
k=n/2
. In
Fix any
p\geq 2
, define
\tilde{W}_{k}:=\frac{W_{k}\sqrt{n}}{\sigma\sqrt{k(n-k)}}
, and let
Z
represent an independent standard normal variable. We first remark that

\mathcal{W}_{p}(W_{k},\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{n,k}^{2})\leq\sigma_{n,k}\mathcal{W}_{p}(\tilde{W}_{k},Z).

We first bound the right hand side. In this goal, we note that, for all
j\geq 1
, the moments of
X_{i}^{j}
can be upper bounded as

\displaystyle\|X_{i}^{j}\|_{p}=\big(\mathbb{E}(|X_{i}|^{jp})\big)^{1/p}\leq\big(\|X_{i}\|_{\infty}^{kp-2}\mathbb{E}(|X_{i}|^{2})\big)^{1/p}\leq\frac{R_{s}^{j}\sigma^{2/p}}{R_{s}^{2/p}}.

(33)

Consider a random index
I\sim\textnormal{Uniform}(\{1,\dots,n\})
and a sequence
(X_{i}^{\prime})_{i\geq 1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}(X_{i})_{i\geq 1}
with

(I,(X_{i}^{\prime})_{i\geq 1},(X_{i})_{i\geq 1})
mutually independent.
Define an exchangeable copy of
\tilde{W}_{k}
,

\displaystyle W^{\prime}_{k}:=\tilde{W}_{k}+(X^{\prime}_{I}-X_{I})\sigma_{n,k}^{-1}\begin{cases}(1-\frac{k}{n})\quad\text{if}\penalty\ I\leq k\\
-\frac{k}{n}\quad\text{otherwise}\end{cases},

(34)

and the exchangeable pair difference,

\displaystyle Y:=\tilde{W}_{k}-W^{\prime}_{j}.

Fix any
\kappa\geq\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n}
and define

\displaystyle M_{n,\kappa}:={\sqrt{1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa}}}.

(35)

A slight modification of Theorem 3 of
[
7
]
shows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{p}(\tilde{W}_{k},Z)\leq

\displaystyle\int^{-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa})}_{0}\|e^{-t}\tilde{W}_{k}-\frac{e^{-2t}}{\sqrt{1-e^{-2t}}}Z\|_{p}dt

\displaystyle+\int_{-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-\frac{R^{2}}{n\kappa})}^{\infty}e^{-t}\|n\mathbb{E}\big(Y|\tilde{W}_{k}\big)-\tilde{W}_{k}\|_{p}dt

\displaystyle+\int_{-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa})}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-2t}\|H_{1}\|_{p}}{\sqrt{1-e^{-2t}}}\|\frac{n}{2}\mathbb{E}\big(Y^{2}|\tilde{W}_{k}\big)-1\|_{p}dt

\displaystyle+\sum_{j\geq 3}\int_{-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa})}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-jt}\|H_{j-1}\|_{p}}{j!(\sqrt{1-e^{-2t}})^{j-1}}n\|\mathbb{E}\big(Y^{j}|\tilde{W}_{k}\big)\|_{p}dt

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\ (a_{0})+(a_{1})+(a_{2})+(a_{3}).

We first bound
(a_{0}).
To this end, fix
\epsilon>0
and select
G\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
independent from
Z
such that
\|\tilde{W}_{k}-G\|_{p}\leq\mathcal{W}_{p}(\tilde{W}_{k},Z)+\epsilon.

By the triangle inequality we have

\displaystyle\int^{-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa})}_{0}\|e^{-t}\tilde{W}_{k}-\frac{e^{-2t}}{\sqrt{1-e^{-2t}}}Z\|_{p}dt

\displaystyle\leq\int^{-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa})}_{0}e^{-t}\|\tilde{W}_{k}-G\|_{p}+\|e^{-t}G-\frac{e^{-2t}}{\sqrt{1-e^{-2t}}}Z\|_{p}dt

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}(1-M_{n,\kappa})\|\tilde{W}_{k}-G\|_{p}+\|Z\|_{p}\int^{-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa})}_{0}\frac{e^{-t}}{\sqrt{1-e^{-2t}}}dt

\displaystyle\leq(1-M_{n,\kappa})\big\{\mathcal{W}_{p}(\tilde{W}_{k},Z)+\epsilon\big\}+\|Z\|_{p}\big[\frac{\pi}{2}-\mathrm{sin}^{-1}(M_{n,\kappa})\big],

where (a) follows from the fact
e^{-t}G-\frac{e^{-2t}}{\sqrt{1-e^{-2t}}}Z\overset{d}{=}\frac{e^{-t}}{\sqrt{1-e^{-2t}}}Z
.
Since
\epsilon>0
was arbitrary, we have

\displaystyle(a_{0})\leq(1-M_{n,\kappa})\mathcal{W}_{p}(\tilde{W}_{k},Z)+\|Z\|_{p}\big[\frac{\pi}{2}-\mathrm{sin}^{-1}(M_{n,\kappa})\big].

(36)

In addition, if
p=2
, by independence of
Z
and
S_{n}
we obtain that

\displaystyle\int^{-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa})}_{0}\|e^{-t}\tilde{W}_{k}-\frac{e^{-2t}}{\sqrt{1-e^{-2t}}}Z\|_{p}dt

\displaystyle\leq\int^{-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa})}_{0}\sqrt{e^{-2t}\|\tilde{W}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{e^{-4t}}{{1-e^{-2t}}}\|Z\|_{2}^{2}}dt

(37)

\displaystyle\leq\int^{-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa})}_{0}\frac{e^{-t}}{\sqrt{1-e^{-2t}}}dt\leq\frac{\pi}{2}-\mathrm{sin}^{-1}(M_{n,\kappa}).

(38)

Therefore, according to
section

I.2
, if
p>2
we have

\displaystyle M_{n,\kappa}\mathcal{W}_{p}(\tilde{W}_{k},Z)\leq

\displaystyle\|Z\|_{p}\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}-\mathrm{sin}^{-1}(\sqrt{1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa}})\Big)+(a_{1})+(a_{2})+(a_{3}).

and if
p=2
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{p}(\tilde{W}_{k},Z)\leq

\displaystyle\|Z\|_{p}\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}-\mathrm{sin}^{-1}(\sqrt{1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa}})\Big)+(a_{1})+(a_{2})+(a_{3}).

We will next bound
(a_{1})
of
section

I.2
.
Note that, since
I\sim\textnormal{Uniform}(\{1,\dots,n\})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big(Y|\tilde{W}_{k}\big)=\frac{\sigma_{n,k}^{-1}}{2n}\sum_{i\leq k}\mathbb{E}(X_{i}-X^{\prime}_{i}|\tilde{W}_{k})-\frac{\sigma_{n,k}^{-1}}{n}\sum_{i\leq k}\mathbb{E}(X_{i}-X^{\prime}_{i}|\tilde{W}_{k})=\frac{1}{n}\tilde{W}_{k},

and hence
\|n\mathbb{E}\big(Y|\tilde{W}_{k}\big)-\tilde{W}_{k}\|_{p}=0
.
Therefore
(a_{1})=0
.

We now turn to bounding
(a_{2})
of
section

I.2
.
By Jensen’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|\frac{n}{2}\mathbb{E}\big(Y^{2}|\tilde{W}_{k}\big)-1\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\|\frac{1}{2\sigma_{n,k}^{2}}\sum_{i\leq k}(1-\frac{k}{n})^{2}(\mathbb{E}(X_{i}^{2})+X_{i}^{2})+\frac{1}{2\sigma_{n,k}^{2}}\sum_{i>k}\frac{k^{2}}{n^{2}}(\mathbb{E}(X_{i}^{2})+X_{i}^{2})-1\|_{p}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8}\|\frac{1}{\sigma_{n,k}^{2}}\sum_{i\leq k}X_{i}^{2}+\frac{1}{\sigma_{n,k}^{2}}\sum_{i>k}\sum_{i\leq n}X_{i}^{2}-1\|_{p}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2n}\|\sum_{i\leq n}\sigma^{-2}X_{i}^{2}-1\|_{p}.

To better bound this we first obtain an upper bound for
\|\sigma^{-2}X_{i}^{2}-1\|_{p}
. In this goal, we remark that
|\sigma^{-2}X_{i}^{2}-1|\overset{a.s.}{\leq}\max(\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1,1\big)
. We will get a different upper bound depending on if
\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1\leq 1
or not.
First suppose
2\geq\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}
.
Since
\mathbb{E}(X_{i}^{2})=\sigma^{2}
, we know
\|\sigma^{-2}X_{i}^{2}-1\|_{p}
is maximized when
\frac{1}{R_{s}^{2}}X_{i}^{2}\sim\textnormal{Bernoulli}(\frac{1}{\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}}).

Hence

\displaystyle\|\sigma^{-2}X_{i}^{2}-1\|_{p}\leq\Big[\frac{\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1}{\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}}\Big]^{1/p}\big((\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1)^{p-1}+1\big)^{1/p}.

(39)

Now suppose
\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}\geq 2
. We instead obtain that

\displaystyle\|\sigma^{-2}X_{i}^{2}-1\|_{p}\leq(\|X_{i}^{2}\sigma^{-2}-1\|_{\infty}^{p-2}\|X_{i}^{2}\sigma^{-2}-1\|_{2}^{2})^{1/p}\leq\big(\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1\big)^{1-1/p}.

(40)

Therefore using the Marcinkiewicz-Zygmund inequality (
lemma

K.3
) we have if
\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}\geq 2

\displaystyle\|\frac{n}{2}\mathbb{E}\big(Y^{2}|\tilde{W}_{k}\big)-1\|_{p}\overset{}{\leq}\frac{1}{2\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{p-1}(\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1)^{1-1/p}

and if
\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}\leq 2
we instead obtain that

\displaystyle\|\frac{n}{2}\mathbb{E}\big(Y^{2}|\tilde{W}_{k}\big)-1\|_{p}

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}\frac{1}{2\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{p-1}\Big[\frac{R_{s}^{2}-1}{R_{s}^{2}}\Big]^{1/p}\big((R_{s}^{2}-1)^{p-1}+1\big)^{1/p}

Moreover using the Rosenthal inequality
lemma

K.2
we find that

\displaystyle\|\frac{n}{2}\mathbb{E}\big(Y^{2}|\tilde{W}_{k}\big)-1\|_{p}\overset{}{\leq}\frac{1}{2\sqrt{n}}\Big[(\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1)^{1-1/p}A_{n,p}^{*}+\sqrt{\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1}A_{p}\Big].

for
\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}\geq 2
and

\displaystyle\|\frac{n}{2}\mathbb{E}\big(Y^{2}|\tilde{W}_{k}\big)-1\|_{p}

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}\frac{1}{2\sqrt{n}}\Big[\frac{R_{s}^{2}-1}{R_{s}^{2}}\Big]^{1/p}\Big[\big((R_{s}^{2}-1)^{p-1}+1\big)^{1/p}A^{*}_{n,p}+\sqrt{R_{s}^{2}-1}A_{p}\Big]

for
\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}\leq 2
.
Alternatively, if
k=n/2
by
[
17
, Eq. (2.8)]
for
p<4
and
[
15
, Thm. 2.6]
for
p\geq 4
, we have the symmetrized estimate

\displaystyle\|\sigma^{-2}\sum_{i\leq n}X_{i}^{2}-1\|_{p}\leq 2^{-1/p}\|\sigma^{-2}\sum_{i\leq n}X_{i}^{2}-(X^{\prime}_{i})^{2}\|_{p}.

Since the random variables
\big(X_{i}^{2}-(X^{\prime}_{i})^{2}\big)_{i\geq 1}
are symmetric, with

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(X_{i}^{2}-(X^{\prime}_{i})^{2})^{2}]\leq\frac{2}{n^{2}}(R_{s}^{2}-\sigma^{2})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{E}[(X_{i}^{2}-(X^{\prime}_{i})^{2})^{p}]\leq\frac{2}{n^{p}}(R_{s}^{2}-\sigma^{2})R_{s}^{2(p-2)},

an improvement on the Marcinkiewicz-Zygmund inequality for symmetric random variables (
lemma

K.8
) implies

\displaystyle\|\sigma^{-2}\sum_{i\leq n}X_{i}^{2}-(X^{\prime}_{i})^{2}\|_{p}\leq\begin{cases}{\sqrt{2}2^{1/p}}{\sqrt{n}}\Big(\frac{\Gamma(\frac{p+1}{2})}{\sqrt{\pi}}\Big)^{1/p}\tilde{R}_{s}^{2(1-2/p)}(\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1)^{1/p}&\ \text{if}\ p\geq 2\\
{\sqrt{2}}\Big(\frac{\Gamma(\frac{p+1}{2})}{\sqrt{\pi}}\Big)^{1/p}{\tilde{R}^{2}}\|\textnormal{Binomial}(n,\frac{2(\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1)}{\tilde{R}_{s}^{4}})\|_{p}&\ \text{if}\ p\geq 4.\end{cases}

Hence we finally obtain that

\displaystyle\qquad\|\frac{n}{2}\mathbb{E}\big(Y^{2}|\tilde{W}_{k}\big)-1\|_{p}

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}D^{k}_{n,p}:=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{n}}\min\begin{cases}\sqrt{p-1}\max\Big((\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1)^{1-1/p},\Big[\frac{(\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1)^{p}+\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1}{\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}}\Big]^{1/p}\Big)\\
\max\Big((\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1)^{1-1/p},\Big[\frac{(\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1)^{p}\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1}{\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}}\Big]^{1/p}\Big)A^{*}_{n,p}+\sqrt{\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1}A_{p}\\
\sqrt{2}\Big(\frac{\Gamma(\frac{p+1}{2})}{\sqrt{\pi}}\Big)^{1/p}\tilde{R}_{s}^{2(1-2/p)}(\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1)^{1/p}\\
\sqrt{2}2^{-1/p}\Big(\frac{\Gamma(\frac{p+1}{2})}{\sqrt{\pi}}\Big)^{1/p}\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\|\textnormal{Binomial}(n,\frac{2(\tilde{R}_{s}^{2}-1)}{\tilde{R}_{s}^{4}})\|_{p/2}}\quad\ \text{if}\ p\geq 4.\end{cases}

This implies that

\displaystyle\int_{-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-\frac{R^{2}}{n\kappa})}^{\infty}\|\frac{n}{2}\mathbb{E}\big((Y)^{2}|\tilde{W}_{k}\big)-1\|_{p}\frac{e^{-2t}}{\sqrt{1-e^{-2t}}}dt

\displaystyle\leq D_{n,p}\int_{-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-\frac{R^{2}}{n\kappa})}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-2t}}{\sqrt{1-e^{-2t}}}dt=D_{n,p}\int^{\sqrt{1-\frac{R^{2}}{n\kappa}}}_{0}\frac{t}{\sqrt{1-t^{2}}}dt=M_{n,\kappa}^{2}D_{n,p}.

Therefore since
H_{1}(Z)=Z
we obtain

\displaystyle(a_{2})

\displaystyle\leq(b_{2,1}^{\kappa,p,\tilde{R}}):=\|Z\|_{p}D_{n,p}M_{n,\kappa}^{2}.

Finally, we turn to bounding
(a_{3})
of
section

I.2
. Since

\displaystyle n\mathbb{E}(Y^{j}|X_{1},\dots,X_{n})=\sigma_{n,k}^{-j}\sum_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}((X_{i}-X^{\prime}_{i})^{j}|X_{1},\dots,X_{n})\quad\text{for all}\quad j\geq 1,

by Jensen’s inequality, we have for all
j\geq 1

\displaystyle n\big\|\mathbb{E}(Y^{j}|\tilde{W}_{k})\big\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\sigma_{n,k}^{-j}\big\|\sum_{i\leq k}(1-\frac{k}{n})^{j}(X_{i}-X^{\prime}_{i})^{j}+\sum_{i\leq k}(\frac{k}{n})^{j}(X_{i}-X^{\prime}_{i})^{j}\big\|_{p}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{n,k}^{-j}2^{-j}\big\|\sum_{i\leq k}(X_{i}-X^{\prime}_{i})^{j}+\sum_{i\leq k}(X_{i}-X^{\prime}_{i})^{j}\big\|_{p}.

We will derive different bounds for odd and even
j
, so we begin by writing

\displaystyle(a_{3})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\geq 3\\
j\penalty\ \rm{is\penalty\ odd}\end{subarray}}\int_{-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa})}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-jt}\|H_{j-1}\|_{p}}{j!(\sqrt{1-e^{-2t}})^{j-1}}n\|\mathbb{E}\big(Y^{j}|\tilde{W}_{k}\big)\|_{p}dt

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\geq 4\\
j\penalty\ \rm{is\penalty\ even}\end{subarray}}\int_{-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa})}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-jt}\|H_{j-1}\|_{p}}{j!(\sqrt{1-e^{-2t}})^{j-1}}n\|\mathbb{E}\big(Y^{j}|\tilde{W}_{k}\big)\|_{p}dt:=(a_{3,1})+(a_{3,2}).

Let
j\geq 3
be an
odd
integer. The random variables
((X_{i}-X^{\prime}_{i})^{j})_{i\geq 1}
are symmetric and therefore have a mean of zero.
Moreover we note that

\displaystyle\|(X_{i}-X^{\prime}_{i})^{j}\|_{p}=\|(Y_{i}-Y^{\prime}_{i})^{j}\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\|\max(Y_{i},Y_{i}^{\prime})^{j-2/p}|Y_{i}-Y^{\prime}_{i}|^{2/p}\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq R^{j-2/p}\||Y_{i}-Y^{\prime}_{i}|^{2/p}\|_{p}\leq{2^{1/p}R^{j-2/p}\sigma^{2/p}}.

Similarly, we also have

\displaystyle\|(X_{i}-X^{\prime}_{i})^{j}\|_{2}\leq{\sqrt{2}R^{j-1}}\sigma.

(41)

Therefore, using
lemmas

K.3
,
K.8
and
K.2

we obtain that

\sigma^{-j}\big\|\sum_{i\leq n}(X_{i}-X^{\prime}_{i})^{j}\big\|_{p}\leq\frac{\tilde{R}^{j-1}}{\sqrt{n}^{j-1}}C_{n,p}
where we set

\displaystyle C_{n,p}:=\min\begin{cases}\tilde{A}_{n,p}\tilde{R}2^{1/p}+\sqrt{2}A_{n,p}\\
{\sqrt{2}}\Big(\frac{\Gamma(\frac{p+1}{2})}{\sqrt{\pi}}\Big)^{1/p}\begin{cases}2^{1/p}\tilde{R}^{1-2/p}\text{\quad if }p<4\\
2^{1/p}\tilde{R}^{1-2/p}\wedge\frac{\tilde{R}}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\|\textnormal{Binomial}(n,\frac{2}{\tilde{R}^{2}})\|_{p/2}}\quad\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}\end{cases}

Hence we obtain that

\displaystyle(a_{3,1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\geq 3\\
odd\end{subarray}}\int_{-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa})}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-tj}\|H_{j-1}\|_{p}}{j!\sqrt{{1-e^{-2t}}}^{j-1}}n\|\mathbb{E}\big(Y^{j}|S_{n}\big)\|_{p}dt

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\geq 3\\
odd\end{subarray}}{C_{n,p}}\int^{\sqrt{1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa}}}_{0}\frac{x^{j-1}\tilde{R}^{j-1}\|H_{j-1}\|_{p}}{j!\sqrt{1-x^{2}}^{j-1}\sqrt{n}^{j-1}}dx

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\geq 1\end{subarray}}C_{n,p}\frac{\tilde{R}^{2j}\|H_{2j}\|_{p}}{n^{j}(2j+1)!}\int^{\sqrt{1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa}}}_{0}\frac{x^{2j}}{(1-x^{2})^{j}}dx

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{=}\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\geq 1\end{subarray}}C_{n,p}\frac{\tilde{R}^{2j}\|H_{2j}\|_{p}}{n(2j+1)!}\int_{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{\kappa}}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{y}}\big(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n}\big)^{j-\frac{1}{2}}dy,

where (a) is obtained by noting that all odd numbers
k
can be written as
2m+1
for an
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and (b) by the change of variables
y=n(1-x^{2})
.

To further upper bound the right-hand side, we will invoke the Hermite polynomial moment bound (
lemma

K.5
)
\|H_{j-1}\|_{p}\leq{\sqrt{p-1}^{j-1}}{\sqrt{j-1!}}
and use two applications of Stirling’s approximation
[
46
]
to conclude that, for all
m\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\}
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{(2m)!}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\sqrt{2\pi(2m)}\cdot(2m/e)^{2m}\cdot\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{12(2m)+1}}}\right)}\geq e^{-19/300}2^{m}m!/(\pi m)^{1/4}.

(42)

These estimates imply that, for all
K_{p}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

\displaystyle(a_{3,1})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{n,p}}{2}\Big\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq K_{p}-1\end{subarray}}\frac{\tilde{R}^{2j}\|H_{2j}\|_{p}}{n(2j+1)!}\int_{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{\kappa}}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{y}}\big(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n}\big)^{j-\frac{1}{2}}dy

\displaystyle\quad+e^{19/300}\pi^{1/4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\geq K_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{2^{-j}\tilde{R}^{2j}(p-1)^{j}k^{1/4}}{(2k+1)n{j!}}\int_{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{\kappa}}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{y}}\big(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n}\big)^{j-\frac{1}{2}}dy\Big\}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\frac{C_{n,p}}{2}\Big\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq K_{p}-1\end{subarray}}\frac{\tilde{R}^{2j}\|H_{2j}\|_{p}}{n(2j+1)!}\int_{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{\kappa}}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{y}}\big(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n}\big)^{j-\frac{1}{2}}dy

\displaystyle\quad+e^{19/300}\pi^{1/4}\frac{K_{p}^{1/4}}{(2K_{p}+1)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\geq K_{p}\end{subarray}}\frac{2^{-j}\tilde{R}^{2j}(p-1)^{j}}{n{j!}}\int_{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{\kappa}}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{y}}\big(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n}\big)^{j-\frac{1}{2}}dy\Big\}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\frac{C_{n,p}}{2}\Big\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq K_{p}-1\end{subarray}}\frac{\tilde{R}^{2j}}{n}\Big(\frac{\|H_{2j}\|_{p}}{(2j+1)!}-\frac{K_{p}^{1/4}2^{-j}(p-1)^{j}e^{19/300}\pi^{1/4}}{(2K_{p}+1)j!}\Big)\int_{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{\kappa}}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{y}}\big(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n}\big)^{j-\frac{1}{2}}dy

\displaystyle\quad+e^{19/300}\pi^{1/4}\frac{K_{p}^{1/4}}{(2K_{p}+1)n}\int_{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{\kappa}}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{y}{n}}}\big[e^{\frac{1}{2}(p-1)\tilde{R}^{2}\big(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n}\big)}-1\big]dy\Big\},

where (a) follows from the fact that
x\rightarrow\frac{x^{1/4}}{(2x+1)}
is decreasing and (b) from the fact that

\displaystyle e^{\frac{1}{2}(p-1)\tilde{R}^{2}(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n})}-1=\sum_{j=1}^{K_{p}-1}\frac{(\tilde{R}^{2}(p-1))^{j}2^{-j}}{j!}\big(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n}\big)^{j}+\sum_{j=K_{p}}^{\infty}\frac{(\tilde{R}^{2}(p-1))^{j}2^{-j}}{j!}\big(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n}\big)^{j}.

Next suppose
j\geq 4
is even. Then
\big((X_{i}-X^{\prime}_{i})^{j}\big)_{i\geq 1}
are almost surely nonnegative. Moreover,

\displaystyle\sigma^{-j}\mathbb{E}((X_{i}-X^{\prime}_{i})^{j})\leq 2\frac{\tilde{R}^{j-2}}{\sqrt{n}^{j}},\qquad\sigma^{-jp}\mathbb{E}((X_{i}-X^{\prime}_{i})^{jp})\leq 2\frac{\tilde{R}^{jp-2}}{\sqrt{n}^{jp}}.

Therefore we can invoke a moment inequality for nonnegative random variables (
lemma

K.7
) to conclude

\displaystyle n\|\mathbb{E}(Y^{j}|\tilde{W}_{k})\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\sigma_{n,k}^{-j}\|\sum_{i\leq n}(X_{i}-X^{\prime}_{i})^{j}\|_{p}\leq\frac{\tilde{R}^{j}}{\sqrt{n}^{j}}\Big\|\textnormal{Binomial}\Big(n,\frac{2}{\tilde{R}^{2}}\Big)\Big\|_{p}.

Moreover by the triangle inequality and
lemma

K.3
the following upper also holds

\displaystyle n\big\|\mathbb{E}(Y^{j}|\tilde{W}_{k})\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\sigma_{n,k}^{-j}\big\|\sum_{i\leq n}(X_{i}-X_{i}^{\prime})^{j}\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq n\sigma_{n,k}^{-j}\mathbb{E}((X_{1}-X^{\prime}_{1})^{j})+\sigma_{n,k}^{-j}\big\|\sum_{i\leq n}(X_{i}-X_{i}^{\prime})^{j}-\mathbb{E}((X_{1}-X^{\prime}_{1})^{j})\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tilde{R}^{j-2}}{\sqrt{n}^{j-2}}\Big[1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{U}_{n,p}\tilde{R}\Big].

If instead
k\neq n/2
we have

\displaystyle n\big\|\mathbb{E}(Y^{j}|\tilde{W}_{k})\|_{p}

\displaystyle\qquad+n\sigma_{n,k}^{-j}\mathbb{E}((X_{1}-X^{\prime}_{1})^{j})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tilde{R}^{j-2}}{\sqrt{n}^{j}}\Big[k\big(\frac{n-k}{n}\big)^{j/2}+(n-k)\big(\frac{k}{n}\big)^{j/2}+\sqrt{p-1}\tilde{R}^{2-2/p}\sqrt{k\big(\frac{n-k}{n}\big)^{j}+(n-k)\big(\frac{k}{n}\big)^{j}}\Big].

Hence we obtain

\displaystyle n\|\mathbb{E}(Y^{j}|\tilde{W}_{k})\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\sigma_{n,k}^{-j}\|\sum_{i\leq n}(X_{i}-X^{\prime}_{i})^{j}\|_{p}\leq\frac{B_{p,n}\tilde{R}^{j-2}}{\sqrt{n}^{j-2}}

for

B_{p,n}:=\min\Big(\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n}\|\textnormal{Binomial}(n,\frac{2}{\tilde{R}^{2}})\|_{p},1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{U}_{n,p}\tilde{R}\Big).

This gives us the upper estimate

\displaystyle\int_{-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa})}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-tj}}{\sqrt{1-e^{-2t}}^{j-1}}n\|\mathbb{E}\big(Y^{j}|S_{n}\big)\|_{p}dt

\displaystyle\leq B_{p,n}\int_{-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa})}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-tj}\tilde{R}^{j-2}}{\sqrt{1-e^{-2t}}^{j-1}\sqrt{n}^{j-2}}dt.

To bound
(a_{3,2})
, it remains to bound

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\geq 4\\
even\end{subarray}}\frac{\|H_{j-1}\|_{p}}{j!}\int_{-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa})}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-tj}\tilde{R}^{j-2}}{\sqrt{1-e^{-2t}}^{j-1}\sqrt{n}^{j-2}}dt

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\geq 3\\
odd\end{subarray}}\frac{\tilde{R}^{j-1}\|H_{j}\|_{p}}{(j+1)!\sqrt{n}^{j-1}}\int^{\sqrt{1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa}}}_{0}\frac{x^{j}}{\sqrt{1-x^{2}}^{j}}dx

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\geq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{\tilde{R}^{2j}\|H_{2j+1}\|_{p}}{(2j+2)!}\int^{\sqrt{1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa}}}_{0}\frac{x^{2j+1}}{\sqrt{1-x^{2}}^{2j+1}n^{j}}dx

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\geq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{\tilde{R}^{2j}\|H_{2j+1}\|_{p}}{(2j+2)!\sqrt{n}}\int_{{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{\kappa}}}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{y}}\Big(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n}\Big)^{j}dy,

where (a) and (c) are obtained by a change of variable, and (b) is a consequence of the fact that every odd number can be written as
2m+1
for an
m\geq 1
.
To upper bound this quantity we will again employ a Hermite polynomial moment bound (
lemma

K.5
),
\|H_{j-1}\|_{p}\leq\sqrt{p-1}^{j-1}\sqrt{j-1!}
, and use Stirling’s approximation to deduce
that, for all
m\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sqrt{(2m+1)!}=\sqrt{2m+1}\sqrt{2m!}\geq\sqrt{2m+1}e^{-19/300}2^{m}m!/(\pi m)^{1/4}.\end{split}

Hence for any
K_{p}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
we obtain that

\displaystyle(a_{3,2})=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\geq 4\\
even\end{subarray}}\frac{\|H_{j-1}\|_{p}}{j!}\int_{-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa})}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-tj}}{\sqrt{1-e^{-2t}}^{j-1}}n\|\mathbb{E}\big(Y^{j}|S_{n}\big)\|_{p}dt

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}\frac{B_{p,n}}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K_{p}>j\geq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{\tilde{R}^{2j}\|H_{2j+1}\|_{p}}{(2j+2)!\sqrt{n}}\int_{{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{\kappa}}}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{y}}\Big(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n}\Big)^{j}dy

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{B_{p,n}}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K_{p}\leq j\end{subarray}}\frac{\tilde{R}^{2j}(p-1)^{k+1/2}}{(k+1)\sqrt{2k+1!}\sqrt{n}}\int_{{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{\kappa}}}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{y}}\Big(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n}\Big)^{j}dy

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}\frac{B_{p,n}}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K_{p}>j\geq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{\tilde{R}^{2j}}{\sqrt{n}}\Big(\frac{\|H_{2j+1}\|_{p}}{(2j+2)!}-\frac{2^{-j}e^{19/300}\pi^{1/4}K_{p}^{1/4}\sqrt{p-1}^{2k+1}}{2(K_{p}+1)\sqrt{2K_{p}+1}j!}\Big)\int_{{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{\kappa}}}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{y}}\Big(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n}\Big)^{j}dy

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{B_{p,n}}{4}e^{19/300}\pi^{1/4}\frac{K_{p}^{1/4}\sqrt{p-1}}{(K_{p}+1)\sqrt{2K_{p}+1}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{\kappa}}}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{y}}\big(e^{\frac{1}{2}(p-1)\tilde{R}^{2}(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n})}-1\big)dy.

Therefore, to conclude, we obtain that

\displaystyle(a_{3})\leq(b_{3,1}^{\kappa,p,\tilde{R}})

(43)

\displaystyle:=\frac{B_{p,n}}{2}\Big\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K_{p}>j\geq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{R^{2k}}{\sqrt{n}}\Big(\frac{\|H_{2j+1}\|_{p}}{(2j+2)!}-\frac{2^{-j}e^{19/300}\pi^{1/4}K_{p}^{1/4}\sqrt{p-1}^{2k+1}}{2(K_{p}+1)\sqrt{2K_{p}+1}j!}\Big)\int_{{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{\kappa}}}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{y}}\Big(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n}\Big)^{j}dy

(44)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{4}e^{19/300}\pi^{1/4}\frac{K_{p}^{1/4}\sqrt{p-1}}{(K_{p}+1)\sqrt{2K_{p}+1}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{\kappa}}}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{y}}\big(e^{\frac{1}{2}(p-1)\tilde{R}^{2}(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n})}-1\big)dy\Big\}

(45)

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{n,p}}{2}\Big\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq K_{p}-1\end{subarray}}\frac{\tilde{R}^{2j}}{n}\Big(\frac{\|H_{2j}\|_{p}}{(2j+1)!}-\frac{K_{p}^{1/4}2^{-j}(p-1)^{j}e^{19/300}\pi^{1/4}}{(2K_{p}+1)j!}\Big)\int_{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{\kappa}}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{y}}\big(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n}\big)^{j-\frac{1}{2}}dy

(46)

\displaystyle\quad+e^{19/300}\pi^{1/4}\frac{K_{p}^{1/4}}{(2K_{p}+1)n}\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{\frac{\kappa}{\tilde{R}^{2}}}\frac{1}{y^{3/2}\sqrt{y-\frac{1}{n}}}\big[e^{\frac{1}{2}(p-1)\tilde{R}^{2}\big(y-\frac{1}{n}\big)}-1\big]dy\Big\}.

(47)

Finally, using
lemma

K.3
we once again note that the following trivial upper-bound also holds

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{p}(W_{k},\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{n,k}^{2}))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{p-1}\sqrt{n}}{2}(\sigma+R).

∎

Lemma I.2
.

For all
p\geq 2
and for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
we have

\mathcal{S}_{p}^{\sigma,R}(n,k)\leq{\hyperref@@ii[KRsig]{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{K_{R,\sigma}}}p}

for

\displaystyle K_{R,\sigma}\triangleq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\sigma+\sqrt{2}R))\vee\Big\{\textstyle\max(\frac{R}{\sigma},\frac{3\sigma}{4}\Big(\sqrt{\widetilde{\hyperref@@ii[Rsig]{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{R_{\sigma}^{2}}}}-1}\frac{\sqrt{e}(2e)^{1/2}}{\sqrt{2}}+\frac{\pi^{1/4}e^{19/300}}{4\sqrt{3}}\big[e^{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{2}}-1\big]\Big)

\displaystyle\textstyle+\sigma\sqrt{2}\Big(1+\log(4)\Big)\Big\{\frac{\tilde{R}^{1-2/p}\sqrt{8\pi}^{1/2}}{3\sqrt{e}}e^{19/300}\pi^{1/4}[e^{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{2}}-1]\Big\}

(
K_{R,\sigma}
)

\displaystyle\textstyle+\sqrt{4}{\sigma}\frac{(\max(\widetilde{\hyperref@@ii[Rsig]{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{R_{\sigma}^{2}}}}-1,1))^{1/2}}{\sqrt{2}}+{\sigma}\tilde{R}{\sqrt{e}(2e)^{1/2}}\frac{\pi^{1/4}e^{19/300}}{4\sqrt{3}}\big[e^{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{2}}-1\big]

\displaystyle\textstyle+\frac{4\sigma\tilde{R}^{2-2/p}}{\sqrt{2n}}\frac{\pi^{1/4}e^{19/300}}{4\sqrt{3}}\big[e^{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{2}}-1\big]+1)\Big\}.

Moreover for any
0<\sigma_{1}<\sigma_{2}\leq\frac{R}{2}
we have

\max_{\sigma\in[\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}]}|\hyperref@@ii[KRsig]{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{K_{R,\sigma}}}|<\infty.

Proof.

If
n+1\geq p
then this was already proven in Lemma 9 of
[
2
]
. For
p>n
, this is a direct consequence of
lemma

I.1
.
∎

Appendix J
Comparing to the bounds in
[
5
]

In this section, under the additional assumption that the random variables
(Y_{i})
have finite support, we compare the thresholds obtained in
algorithm

2
to the ones obtained in
[
5
]
. As the bounds in
[
5
]
depend on constants that are not given in closed form, instead those constants are the solution of inequalities. We first have to derive those constants. As a remainder we denote
X_{i}=Y_{i}-\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}).

In this subsection, we will assume that the random variables
(X_{i})
take values in a finite alphabet
\mathcal{A}
that is not allowed to contain
0
, have variance
\sigma^{2}=1
and satisfy
\mathbb{E}(X_{1}^{3})=0
. We introduce the following notations

•

\mathcal{D}=\{b-a:a,b\in\mathcal{A}\}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathcal{D}^{+}=\mathcal{D}\cap[0,\infty),

•

Define
q=|\mathcal{D}^{+}|+1
,
D=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{d\in\mathcal{D}^{+}}d,
and
Q:=\sum_{d\in\mathcal{D}^{+}}d^{2}
.

•

W_{k,d}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\leq k\\
j>n\end{subarray}}(X_{i}-X_{j})\mathbb{I}(|X_{i}-X_{j}|=d)
.

Theorem J.1

(Theorem 2.2 of
[
5
]
)
.

For
n\geq 1
, let
(Y_{i})
be random variables that satisfy
Assumption
and that are in addition assumed to take values in
\mathcal{A}\subset\mathbb{R}
. Let
\gamma^{2}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}^{2}
. Assume that
\sigma=1
.Then for all
\nu>0
there exist positive constants
c_{1},c_{2}
and
\theta_{2}
depending only on
\mathcal{A}
and
\nu
such that for any integer
n\geq 1
, an integer
k
such that
|2k-n|\leq 1
, and any
\eta\geq\nu
, it is possible to construct a version of
W_{k}
and a Gaussian random variable
\tilde{Z}_{k}
with mean 0 and variance
k(n-k)/n
on the same probability space such that for all
\theta\leq\theta_{2}
,

\mathbb{E}\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\theta\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|W_{k}-\eta\tilde{Z}_{k}}}\right|\big|\mathcal{U}(X_{1:n})}}\right)\leq\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(3+\frac{c_{1}\theta^{2}S_{n}^{2}}{n}+c_{2}\theta^{2}n\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\gamma^{2}-\eta^{2}}}\right)^{2}}}\right).

(48)

Corollary J.1.1
.

Suppose the assumptions of
theorem

J.1
hold. Then the bound in
eq.

48
holds for all
\nu>0
with the following explicit constants:

c_{1}=\frac{3C}{2}q,\quad c_{2}=2C,

where

C=\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{R_{s}^{2}}{2}+\frac{1}{8}Q}}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{4}+3\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R_{s}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}Q}}\right)}}\right),

and where
\theta_{2}:=\theta_{3}\wedge\theta_{4}
, with
\theta_{4}>0
satisfying

2\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(D\theta_{4}+2C\theta_{4}^{2}}}\right)\leq e,

and
\theta_{3}
defined via

2Cq\theta_{3}^{2}=\alpha_{1},\quad\text{with $\alpha_{1}$ satisfying}\quad\frac{1}{1-4\alpha_{1}R_{s}^{2}}\leq\frac{9}{8}.

Proof.

The inequality at the end of the proof of Theorem 2.2 (page 16) in
[
5
]
states that
the constants
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
in
eq.

48
can be chosen to be

c_{1}=\frac{3C}{2}q,\quad c_{2}=2C.

We now derive the expression for
C
. In the same proof, it is stated that for
k
such that
|2k-n|\leq 1
, the following inequality holds for some constant
C
depending uniquely on
\mathcal{A}
and
\nu
:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(T-\sigma^{2}}}\right)^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n}{k(n-k)\nu^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R_{s}^{2}/2+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}-\sigma^{2}}}\right|+R_{s}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|S_{k}}}\right|+\sum_{d\in\mathcal{D}^{+}}\frac{d}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|W_{k,d}}}\right|+C_{0}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+n\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\gamma^{2}-\eta^{2}}}\right)^{2}+\frac{S_{k}^{2}}{k}+\sum_{d\in\mathcal{D}^{+}}\frac{W_{k,d}^{2}}{k}}}\right),

where
C_{0}
is defined in (2.26) of
[
5
]
and can be taken as

C_{0}:=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{d\in\mathcal{D}^{+}}d^{2}.

Applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{n}{k(n-k)\nu^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R_{s}^{2}/2+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{k(n-k)}{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\gamma^{2}-\eta^{2}}}\right)}}\right|+R_{s}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|S_{k}}}\right|+\sum_{d\in\mathcal{D}^{+}}\frac{d}{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|W_{k,d}}}\right|+C_{0}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{n}{k(n-k)\nu^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((R_{s}^{2}/2+C_{0})^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{k(n-k)}{n}}}\right)^{2}\frac{1}{n}+R_{s}^{2}k+\sum_{d\in\mathcal{D}^{+}}\frac{d^{2}}{4}k}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+n(\gamma^{2}-\eta^{2})^{2}+\frac{1}{k}|S_{k}|^{2}+\sum_{d\in\mathcal{D}^{+}}\frac{W_{k,d}^{2}}{k}}}\right).

Recall that we denote
Q:=\sum_{d\in\mathcal{D}^{+}}d^{2}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{n}{k(n-k)}(R_{s}^{2}/2+\frac{1}{8}Q)^{2}+\frac{k(n-k)}{n}\frac{1}{n}+\frac{n}{n-k}R_{s}^{2}+\frac{n}{n-k}\frac{1}{4}Q}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{n}{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor(n-\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor)}(R_{s}^{2}/2+\frac{1}{8}Q)^{2}+\frac{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor(n-\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor)}{n}\frac{1}{n}+\frac{n}{n-\lceil\frac{n}{2}\rceil}R_{s}^{2}+\frac{n}{n-\lceil\frac{n}{2}\rceil}\frac{1}{4}Q}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2(R_{s}^{2}/2+\frac{1}{8}Q)^{2}+\frac{1}{4}+3\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R_{s}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}Q}}\right)}}\right).

Therefore, we can take

\displaystyle C=\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2(R_{s}^{2}/2+\frac{1}{8}Q)^{2}+\frac{1}{4}+3\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R_{s}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}Q}}\right)}}\right).

The value of
\theta_{2}
follows directly from the proof of Theorem 2.2 and Lemma 2.9 of
[
5
]
.
∎

Theorem J.2

(Lemma 4.1 of
[
5
]
)
.

Assume that the random variables
(X_{i})
satisfy the conditions of
theorem

J.1
and are in addition such that
\mathbb{E}(X_{1}^{3})=0
. There exists a constant
A
such that there exists a constant
\lambda>0
such that for any positive integer
n
, it is possible to construct a version of the sequence
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S_{k}}}\right)_{0\leq k\leq n}
and Gaussian random variables
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Z_{k}}}\right)_{0\leq k\leq n}
with mean zero and
\operatorname{Cov}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Z_{i},Z_{j}}}\right)=i\wedge j
on the same probability space such that

\mathbb{E}\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|S_{n}-Z_{n}}}\right|}}\right)\leq A

and

\mathbb{E}\exp\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lambda\max_{0\leq k\leq n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|S_{k}-Z_{k}}}\right|}}\right)\leq A\exp(A\log n).

(49)

Lemma J.3

(Corollary to Lemma 4.1 of
[
5
]
)
.

Suppose that the assumptions of
theorem

J.2
hold. Then
eq.

49
holds with the following constants:
Let
C
,
K_{1}
,
K_{2}
, and
\lambda_{0}
be as in Theorem 1.4, for
\nu=\min_{a\in\mathcal{A}}|a|
.
Let
A\geq 8
be such that

8^{1/2}\Big[\exp(C\log n)+4\exp(C_{1}\log n)\Big]^{1/2}\leq A\exp(A\log n),

where

C_{1}:=2+8(R_{s}+1)\sqrt{2\pi n}\lambda.

Let
\lambda
be

\lambda=\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\frac{\theta_{1}}{2},\frac{\lambda_{0}}{4},\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}R\sqrt{K_{1}}},\frac{1}{{2}R_{s}^{2}},\frac{1}{R_{s}+1}}}\right\},

where

\theta_{1}=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{3}}\min\{\vartheta_{\ell\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\epsilon^{2}-1}}\right)},\vartheta_{\ell\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\epsilon}}\right)}\},

\quad K_{1}=8c_{1},\quad K_{2}=18c_{2}\quad\text{ and }\quad\lambda_{0}=\sqrt{\frac{\alpha_{1}}{32c_{1}}}\wedge\frac{\theta_{2}}{2}\wedge\frac{\theta_{5}}{\sqrt{72c_{2}}}.

Here,
\theta_{5}
is the unique positive solution to

\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-R_{s}^{4}\theta^{2}/2}}=\frac{4}{3}.

Moreover, the following inequality holds:

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\max_{0\leq k\leq n}|S_{k}-Z_{k}|\geq\lambda^{-1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(A\log n-\log\alpha+\log A}}\right)}}\right)\leq\alpha.

(50)

Proof.

The choice of constants follows directly from the proof of Lemma 4.1 in
[
5
]
, and by tracing the definitions of all the constants involved.
Equation

50
follows by applying the Chernoff bound to
eq.

49
.
∎

We compare our bound with that of
[
5
]
for sample sizes
n=2^{L}
with
L\in\{1,\dots,14\}
for
\alpha=0.05
. For each
R\in\{4,6,8,10\}
, the random variables
X_{i}
’s are sampled i.i.d. from the symmetric finite set
\mathcal{A}_{N}:=\{-R/2,-R/2+R/(N-1),\dots,R/2\}
for some
N>R
. We choose the distribution so that
X_{i}
has mean zero, unit variance, and zero third moment.

with

Appendix K
Additional lemmas

Lemma K.1

(Rosenthal’s inequality with explicit constants)
.

Let
(\tilde{X}_{i})_{i\geq 1}
be a sequence of centered i.i.d. observations. If
\|\tilde{X}_{1}\|_{p}<\infty
for some
p\geq 2
, then

\textstyle\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\leq n}\tilde{X}_{i}\|_{p}\leq(\frac{p}{2}+1)n^{1/p-1/2}\|\tilde{X}_{1}\|_{p}+2^{1/p}\sqrt{p/2+1}e^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}\|\tilde{X}_{1}\|_{2}.

(51)

Proof.

According to
[
38
, Thm. 2]
we have

\textstyle\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\leq n}\tilde{X}_{i}\|_{p}^{p}\leq\inf_{c>\frac{p}{2}}c^{p}n^{1-\frac{p}{2}}\|\tilde{X}_{1}||_{p}^{p}+pc^{p/2}e^{c}B(\frac{p}{2},c-\frac{p}{2})\|\tilde{X}_{1}\|_{2}^{p},

(52)

where
B(\cdot,\cdot)
is the Beta function.
The choice
c=\frac{p}{2}+1
yields

\textstyle\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\leq n}\tilde{X}_{i}\|_{p}^{p}

\textstyle\leq\big(\frac{p}{2}+1)^{p}n^{1-\frac{p}{2}}\|\tilde{X}_{1}||_{p}^{p}+p(\frac{p}{2}+1)^{p/2}e^{\frac{p}{2}+1}B(\frac{p}{2},1)\|\tilde{X}_{1}\|_{2}^{p}

(53)

\textstyle=(\frac{p}{2}+1)^{p}n^{1-\frac{p}{2}}||\tilde{X}_{1}||_{p}^{p}+2(\frac{p}{2}+1)^{\frac{p}{2}}e^{\frac{p}{2}+1}||\tilde{X}_{1}||_{2}^{p}.

(54)

The subadditivity of the
p
-th root now implies the result.
∎

Lemma K.2

(Generalization of Rosenthal inequality with explicit constants)
.

The following inequality holds for all
p>2
:

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{1}{2n}\Big(k\sum_{k<i\leq n}(X_{i}^{2}-1)+(n-k)\sum_{1\leq i\leq k}(X_{i}^{2}-1)\Big)\Big\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{k(n-k)}}{2\sqrt{n}}\min\begin{cases}\sqrt{p-1}(R^{2}_{s}-1)^{1-1/p}\\
A_{p}\sqrt{R^{2}_{s}-1}+A^{*}_{n,p}(R^{2}_{s}-1)^{1-1/p}.\end{cases}

where recall that
A_{p}:=2^{1/p}\sqrt{p/2+1}e^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}
and
A_{n,p}^{*}:=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p}{2}+1}}\right)n^{1/p-1/2}
.

Proof.

Adaptation of proof from
[
38
]
.
∎

Lemma K.3

(Theorem 2.1 from
[
45
]
: Marcinkiewicz-Zygmund type inequality for martingales)
.

Let
p>2
and
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S_{n}}}\right)_{n\geq 0}
be a sequence of random variables in
\mathbb{L}^{p}
. Set
X_{k}=S_{k}-S_{k-1}
. Assume that
\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(X_{k}\mid S_{k-1}}}\right)=0
a.s.for any positive
k
. Then

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|S_{n}}}\right\|_{p}^{2}\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|S_{0}}}\right\|_{p}^{2}+(p-1)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|X_{1}}}\right\|_{p}^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|X_{2}}}\right\|_{p}^{2}+\cdots+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|X_{n}}}\right\|_{p}^{2}}}\right).

Lemma K.4

(Lemma 6 from
[
7
]
)
.

Let
Z
be a normal random variable and let
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M_{\alpha}}}\right)_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Then,

\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\sum M_{\alpha}H_{\alpha}(Z)}}\right\|^{p}}}\right]^{2/p}\leq\sum\max(1,p-1)^{|\alpha|}\alpha!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|M_{\alpha}}}\right\|^{2}.

Lemma K.5

(Bound on the norm of the Hermite polynomials)
.

Let
H_{k}(x)\triangleq e^{x^{2}/2}\frac{\partial^{k}}{\partial x}e^{-x^{2}/2}
. Shorthand
H_{K}\triangleq H_{k}(Z)
. Then the following holds for all
k,p\in\mathbb{N}
:

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|H_{k}}}\right\|_{p}\leq\sqrt{k!}{\sqrt{p-1}^{k}}.

Lemma K.6

(Theorem 1 of
[
40
]
)
.

For
p\geq 4
, the following inequality holds:

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\leq k\\
j>k\end{subarray}}(Y_{i}-Y_{j})^{\ell}\Big\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{i\leq n}\mathbb{E}[D_{i}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]\Big)^{p/2}\Big]^{1/p}+\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i\leq n}D_{i}^{p}\Big]^{1/p}\Big\}

(55)

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\Big\{\sqrt{\sum_{i\leq n}\Big\|\mathbb{E}[D_{i}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]\Big\|_{p/2}}+\Big(\sum_{i\leq n}\|D_{i}\|_{p}^{p}\Big)^{1/p}\Big\},

(56)

where
C_{p}:=2\sqrt{2}\Big(\frac{p}{4}+1\Big)^{1/p}(1+\frac{p}{\log(p/2)})
.

Lemma K.7
.

[Lemma 13 of
[
2
]
] Let
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{X}_{i}}}\right)_{i\geq 1}
be a sequence of i.i.d. random variables that are almost surely nonnegative. If, for some
p\geq 2
,
\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{X}_{1}}}\right)\leq a
and
\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{X}_{i}^{p}}}\right)\leq b
for
a,b>0
, then

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{X}_{i}}}\right\|_{p}\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{b}{a}}}\right)^{1/(p-1)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\operatorname{Binomial}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{a^{p}}{b}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}}}\right)}}\right\|_{p}.

Lemma K.8

(Improved Marcinkiewicz-Zygmund inequality for symmetric random variables)
.

Suppose
(\tilde{X}_{i})_{i\geq 1}
are symmetric centered i.i.d. observations admitting a finite
p
-th absolute moment for some
p\geq 2
. Then

\displaystyle\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\leq n}\tilde{X}_{i}\|_{p}\leq\sqrt{2}\Big(\frac{\Gamma(\frac{p+1}{2})}{\sqrt{\pi}}\Big)^{1/p}\sqrt{n}\|\tilde{X}_{1}\|_{p}.

(57)

If
p\geq 4
,
\mathbb{E}(\tilde{X}_{i}^{2})\leq\tilde{\sigma}^{2}
, and
\mathbb{E}(|\tilde{X}_{i}|^{p})\leq b_{p}
, we also have

\displaystyle\|\sum_{i\leq n}\tilde{X}_{i}\|_{p}\leq\sqrt{2}\Big(\frac{\Gamma(\frac{p+1}{2})}{\sqrt{\pi}}\Big)^{1/p}\Big[\frac{b_{p}}{\tilde{\sigma}^{2}}\Big]^{1/(p-2)}\sqrt{\|\textnormal{Binomial}(n,(\frac{\tilde{\sigma}^{p}}{b_{p}})^{2/(p-2)})\|_{p/2}}.

(58)

Lemma K.9
.

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and all
p\geq 2
the following holds

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{p}(S_{n},\mathcal{N}(0,n\sigma^{2}))

\displaystyle\leq s^{R}_{p}(n,\sigma):=\sqrt{p-1}\sqrt{n}(R+\sigma)\wedge\tilde{\omega}_{p}^{R}(\sigma,n),

where

\displaystyle\sigma^{-1}\tilde{\omega}_{p}^{R}(\sigma,n)

\displaystyle:=\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\kappa\leq\tilde{R}^{2}/n\\
K_{p}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{\sqrt{n}}{M_{n,\kappa}\mathbb{I}(p\neq 2)+\mathbb{I}(p=2)}\Big\{\|Z\|_{p}\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}-\arcsin(\sqrt{1-\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{n\kappa}})\Big)+\|Z\|_{p}D_{n,p}M_{n,\kappa}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{B_{p,n}}{2}\Big\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K_{p}>j\geq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{R^{2k}}{\sqrt{n}}\Big(\frac{\|H_{2j+1}\|_{p}}{(2j+2)!}-\frac{2^{-j}e^{19/300}\pi^{1/4}K_{p}^{1/4}\sqrt{p-1}^{2k+1}}{2(K_{p}+1)\sqrt{2K_{p}+1}j!}\Big)\int_{{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{\kappa}}}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{y}}\Big(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n}\Big)^{j}dy

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{4}e^{19/300}\pi^{1/4}\frac{K_{p}^{1/4}\sqrt{p-1}}{(K_{p}+1)\sqrt{2K_{p}+1}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{\kappa}}}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{y}}\big(e^{\frac{1}{2}(p-1)\tilde{R}^{2}(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n})}-1\big)dy\Big\}

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{n,p}}{2}\Big\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq K_{p}-1\end{subarray}}\frac{\tilde{R}^{2j}}{n}\Big(\frac{\|H_{2j}\|_{p}}{(2j+1)!}-\frac{K_{p}^{1/4}2^{-j}(p-1)^{j}e^{19/300}\pi^{1/4}}{(2K_{p}+1)j!}\Big)\int_{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{\kappa}}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{y}}\big(\frac{1}{y}-\frac{1}{n}\big)^{j-\frac{1}{2}}dy

\displaystyle\quad+e^{19/300}\pi^{1/4}\frac{K_{p}^{1/4}}{(2K_{p}+1)n}\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{\frac{\kappa}{\tilde{R}^{2}}}\frac{1}{y^{3/2}\sqrt{y-\frac{1}{n}}}\big[e^{\frac{1}{2}(p-1)\tilde{R}^{2}\big(y-\frac{1}{n}\big)}-1\big]dy\Big\}\Big\}.

Proof.

Lemma 8 of
[
2
]
establishes that

\mathcal{W}_{p}(S_{n},\mathcal{N}(0,n\sigma^{2}))\leq\tilde{\omega}_{p}^{R}(\sigma,n).

Now by the triangular inequality we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{p}(S_{n},\mathcal{N}(0,n\sigma^{2}))

\displaystyle\leq\|S_{n}\|_{p}+\|\mathcal{N}(0,n\sigma^{2})\|_{p}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\sqrt{p-1}\sqrt{n}(R+\sigma),

where (a) is a consequence of
lemma

K.3
and
lemma

K.5
. ∎

Lemma K.10
.

For all
p\geq 2
and for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
we have

s^{R}_{p}(n,\sigma)\leq{K^{*}_{R,\sigma}p}

for

\displaystyle K^{*}_{R,\sigma}\triangleq

\displaystyle(\sigma+R))\vee\Big\{\textstyle\max(\frac{R}{\sigma},\frac{3\sigma}{4}\Big(\sqrt{\widetilde{\hyperref@@ii[Rsig]{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{R_{\sigma}^{2}}}}-1}\frac{\sqrt{e}(2e)^{1/2}}{\sqrt{2}}+\frac{\pi^{1/4}e^{19/300}}{4\sqrt{3}}\big[e^{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{2}}-1\big]\Big)

\displaystyle\textstyle+\sigma\sqrt{2}\Big(1+\log(4)\Big)\Big\{\frac{\tilde{R}^{1-2/p}\sqrt{8\pi}^{1/2}}{3\sqrt{e}}e^{19/300}\pi^{1/4}[e^{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{2}}-1]\Big\}

(
K_{R,\sigma}
)

\displaystyle\textstyle+\sqrt{4}{\sigma}\frac{(\max(\widetilde{\hyperref@@ii[Rsig]{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{R_{\sigma}^{2}}}}-1,1))^{1/2}}{\sqrt{2}}+{\sigma}\tilde{R}{\sqrt{e}(2e)^{1/2}}\frac{\pi^{1/4}e^{19/300}}{4\sqrt{3}}\big[e^{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{2}}-1\big]

\displaystyle\textstyle+\frac{4\sigma\tilde{R}^{2-2/p}}{\sqrt{2n}}\frac{\pi^{1/4}e^{19/300}}{4\sqrt{3}}\big[e^{\frac{\tilde{R}^{2}}{2}}-1\big]+1)\Big\}.

Moreover for any
0<\sigma_{1}<\sigma_{2}\leq\frac{R}{2}
, we have

\max_{\sigma\in[\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}]}|K^{\star}_{R,\sigma}|<\infty.

Proof.

The proof is a direct consequence of Lemma 9
[
2
]
.
∎

Lemma K.11
.

For all
k\leq n
,
\alpha>0
,
R>0
and
\sigma>0
, let
(\Delta_{k}(\alpha,R,\sigma))_{k}
and
(\mathcal{D}_{k}(\alpha,R,\sigma))_{k}
denote the outputs of
algorithm

1
and
algorithm

2
, respectively. Then, for all
k\leq n
, the following functions are non-decreasing on
[2\sigma,\infty)
:

R\mapsto\mathcal{D}_{k}(\alpha,R,\sigma)\textrm{ and }R\mapsto\Delta_{k}(\alpha,R,\sigma).

Proof.

We remark that
\mathcal{D}_{k}(\alpha,R,\sigma)
and
\Delta_{k}(\alpha,R,\sigma)
are non-decreasing functions of
(\omega_{p}^{R}(n,\sigma))
and
(s_{p}^{R}(n,\sigma))
, respectively. Moreover, by
theorems

C.15
and
K.9
, the functions

R\mapsto\omega_{p}^{R}(n,\sigma)\textrm{ and }R\mapsto s_{p}^{R}(n,\sigma)

are increasing on
[2\sigma,\infty).
The desired result directly follows.
∎

Lemma K.12
.

Assume that the conditions of
proposition

3.1
hold and define
\tilde{R}_{k}:=R/\hat{\sigma}_{k}^{L}
then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\exists k\leq n\penalty\ \textrm{s.t.}\penalty\ |S_{k}-Z_{k}|\geq\hat{\sigma}_{k}^{U}\penalty\ \mathcal{D}_{k}(\alpha,\tilde{R}_{k},1)\textrm{ and }\sigma\in\bigcap_{k=1}^{\infty}[\hat{\sigma}_{k}^{L},\hat{\sigma}_{k}^{U}]\Big)\leq\alpha.

Similarly we also have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\exists k\leq n\penalty\ \textrm{s.t.}\penalty\ |W_{k}-Z_{k}|\geq\hat{\sigma}_{k}^{U}\penalty\ \Delta_{k}(\alpha,\tilde{R}_{k},1)\textrm{ and }\sigma\in\bigcap_{k=1}^{\infty}[\hat{\sigma}_{k}^{L},\hat{\sigma}_{k}^{U}]\Big)\leq\alpha.

Proof.

Define
\tilde{W}_{k}:=\sigma^{-1}W_{k}
and
\tilde{S}_{k}:=\sigma^{-1}S_{k}.
Using
theorems

2.4
and
2.3
we know that there exists centered Gaussian vectors
(\tilde{Z}^{*}_{k}),(Z^{*}_{k})
that have the same variance as respectively
(\tilde{W}_{k})
and
(\tilde{S}_{k})
such that

\mathbb{P}\Big(\exists k\leq n\penalty\ \textrm{s.t.}\penalty\ |\tilde{S}_{k}-Z^{*}_{k}|\geq\mathcal{D}_{k}(\alpha,\frac{R}{\sigma},1)\Big)\leq\alpha

and

\mathbb{P}\Big(\exists k\leq n\penalty\ \textrm{s.t.}\penalty\ |\tilde{W}_{k}-\tilde{Z}^{*}_{k}|\geq\Delta_{k}(\alpha,\frac{R}{\sigma},1)\Big)\leq\alpha.

Define
Z_{k}:=\sigma Z_{k}^{*}
and
\tilde{Z}_{k}:=\sigma\tilde{Z}_{k}^{*}
. We remark that
(Z_{k})
and
(\tilde{Z}_{k})
are centered Gaussian vectors that have the same variance that
(S_{k})
and
(W_{k}).
Moreover we note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\exists k\leq n\penalty\ \textrm{s.t.}\penalty\ |S_{k}-Z_{k}|\geq\sigma\mathcal{D}_{k}(\alpha,\frac{R}{\sigma},1)\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\exists k\leq n\penalty\ \textrm{s.t.}\penalty\ |\tilde{S}_{k}-Z^{*}_{k}|\geq\mathcal{D}_{k}(\alpha,\frac{R}{\sigma},1)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\alpha.

Similarly we have that

\mathbb{P}\Big(\exists k\leq n\penalty\ \textrm{s.t.}\penalty\ |\tilde{W}_{k}-\tilde{Z}^{*}_{k}|\geq\Delta_{k}(\alpha,\frac{R}{\sigma},1)\Big)\leq\alpha.

According to
lemma

K.11
we know that
R\rightarrow\Delta_{k}(\alpha,R,1)
is an increasing function of
R
on
[2,\infty)
. Hence, the following holds

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\exists k\leq n\penalty\ \textrm{s.t.}\penalty\ |S_{k}-Z_{k}|\geq\hat{\sigma}_{k}^{U}\penalty\ \mathcal{D}_{k}(\alpha,\tilde{R}_{k},1)\textrm{ and }\sigma\in\bigcap_{k=1}^{\infty}[\hat{\sigma}_{k}^{L},\hat{\sigma}_{k}^{U}]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\exists k\leq n\penalty\ \textrm{s.t.}\penalty\ |S_{k}-Z_{k}|\geq\sigma\penalty\ \mathcal{D}_{k}(\alpha,R/\sigma,1)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\alpha.

Similarly we can prove that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\exists k\leq n\penalty\ \textrm{s.t.}\penalty\ |W_{k}-Z_{k}|\geq\hat{\sigma}_{k}^{U}\penalty\ \Delta_{k}(\alpha,\tilde{R}_{k},1)\textrm{ and }\sigma\in\bigcap_{k=1}^{\infty}[\hat{\sigma}_{k}^{L},\hat{\sigma}_{k}^{U}]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\alpha.

∎
